# BUSBAR 1.6.0 — THE PLAN FOR DONE

## PATH TO DEV-GREEN (ARCHITECT, stock take 2026-09-30) — the ONE ordered plan

Order (OWNER 2026-09-30): **CODE → DEV-GREEN → PERF → fixes → DEV-GREEN again.** Two documents only:
the spec (what 1.6.0 IS) and this TODO (how we get there). Every item below this section is backlog
feeding these six phases; this section decides the order. Measured on `origin/predev` dac216977.

**Where we really are (measured, not claimed).** The 7 kind ABIs exist at their locked versions and
the per-kind dispatchers are built — but the running binary loads no plugin through them (`main.rs`
builds the dispatcher; `load_linked`/`load_dropped` have no production caller), the open-time refusal
(`busbar-core-connector/src/lib.rs` `NO_TRANSPORT_YET`, "the connector refuses every open") is replaced by a
boot/declare-time refusal of unserved schemes (spec Part 2 #50; PRs #286, #295), no plane is served through a door,
the HOT (7,060 lines) and JSON (4,592) lanes and ~7,800 lines of old loaders are alive, 32 crates sit in
the repo (target 15), and 5 of the 16 populated plugin repos have their memory-ABI door on `dev`
(export-file, -otlp, -prometheus, -webhook, secret-vault; measured by `measure.sh` against each repo's
`dev` source); the 11 plane/transport/hook-ranking/store-memory repos are still empty. Most of the missing
code exists on unlanded branches: the path is landing, the switch-over, and deletion.

**P1 — LAND THE CORE STACK.** In order, each its own small train (≤3 branches; `$` alone):
KIND-SHARE A → WIRE-AUTH → CONNECTOR-19 → HEAD-FIELDS → BOOT-CHAIN; then WIRE-STORE, WIRE-HOOK,
WIRE-SECRET, WIRE-EXPORT, K1 (plane driver + serve), K2 + the money chain (MONEY-CHAIN r2-d, then the
UNITS_FLOOR `$`: **LANDED** on predev, 6da491ba9 PR #168), H2/U10 host services (+ claim_own). *Exit:* the connector opens real connections
(no NULL conn op), framed request/reply is served over `DeclaredConns`, every kind's rows carry conns.

**P2 — SWITCH-OVER.** The composition root loads EVERY plugin — compiled-in or dropped-in — through
the one dispatcher (`load_linked` / `load_dropped`). Delete the HOT/JSON lanes, the old loaders, the
cold entry point and the protocol-named kernel features; TLS lives only in the connector (drop rustls
from the kernel and the http/ws crates). *Exit:* compiled-in ≡ dropped-in for all 7 kinds; old-lane
lines = 0; ~~a both-ways conformance proof per kind~~ a published conformance suite per kind that each real plugin repo runs in its own CI (OWNER 2026-10-03 (no test plugins)); a 1.5.5 JSON plugin is refused at boot (RED).
*Kernel deletion (measured on predev 9cbd0288a: kernel src 102,961 raw non-test lines; ~47k firm to delete, ~13k to rule):*
D0 now: mask.rs, drain.rs and settled-crate dead code (zero callers). D1 with auth-split + K2: kernel and
kernel-identity egress_auth, identity/carrier.rs, the auth carrier list (same train as the plugin path).
D2 after the plane folds flip: plane_host (keep trust, guard), proto, proxy, failover, ir/handlers/engine_facade,
store/planes, plane registry/approvals/cost/observe, plane-specific config and config_validate. D3 after the
connector and transports own the wire: egress engine, tls.rs, router.rs and route seams, ingress framing,
topology/net_guard/sigv4. D4 cleanliness moves: admin contract/schema/taxonomy/verbs/ratelimit → core-admin,
auth/token.rs and oauth_as/config → oauth2, metrics → export-prometheus, hooks wire/plugin/scrape, plugin
diagnostics → Statements. D5: test_support out of kernel src — CLOSED 2026-09-30: already billed as test (0 code / 3210 test lines; the module is `cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))` and the loc meter follows that gate), so a physical move changes no ceiling. Ruled 2026-09-30 (RULE-CHECK, oracle-checked): proxy/reqlog DELETE (in-memory, no reader, 0 cells; re-home the
"one terminal per unit" test witness); calllog MOVE to the mcp plane over the generic journal append, sink folds into
audit::journal, after FOLD-MCP lands; plane/auditlog KEEP (GET /audit, effects.audit) and relocate out of plane/ to core
audit; kernel-audit/legacy DELETE (DELETE-A); config/migrate+overlay KEEP (config.migrate, overlay cells);
governance budget duplicate DELETE (DEL-BUDGET).

**P3 — PLANES.** Finish the llm, mcp, a2a and streaming folds; make decisions reachable; serve each
plane through its door; delete `busbar-{llm,mcp,a2a,voice}`. *Exit:* 5 planes served as plugins; the
oracle byte-green vs 1.5.5; each new plane's conformance rig green.

**P4 — PLUGIN FLEET.** Every remaining plugin on the memory ABI with ~~a both-ways conformance proof~~ the published conformance suite green in its own CI (OWNER 2026-10-03 (no test plugins));
all repos exact twins via `cargo xtask fleet sync` (FLEET-TWIN); pins at predev. *Exit:* every §9
roster repo has its door on `dev`; `fleet check` clean.

**P5 — EXTRACTION TO ~~15~~ 14 CRATES (OWNER 2026-10-03 (Q-A)).** Order: oauth2 → `busbar-core-oauth2` rename; stdio; tcp; ws; grpc;
http; store-memory; hook-ranking; plane-decisions; delete plane-example; delete mcp, a2a, voice, llm
(extracting each plane after its legacy crate goes); ~~delete hook-test-plugin~~ (hook-test-plugin is a test plugin: it is deleted, not extracted; OWNER 2026-10-03 (no test plugins)). Recipe per crate:
filter-repo the crate's history into `<kind>-<name>/`, add the `-plugin` cdylib crate, `fleet sync`
the skeleton, pin in busbar (one git dep + dev-dep at an exact commit, one `[patch]` row for
busbar-contract, `deny.toml` allow-list), and DELETE the crate's rows from busbar's ledgers in the same
commit (its gates run in the plugin repo's CI via `plugin-ci.yml`). The kind exemplars
(`pinned_exemplars`) and the config-schema reader (`pinned_plugin_source`) keep reading pinned
checkouts. ws/http move only after CONNECTOR-19's UPGRADE_SECURE and the rustls drop. *Exit:* 15
crates, zero plugin source in busbar.

**P6 — GATES & RELEASE HYGIENE.** `scripts/verify-1.6.0-done.sh` green on ONE sha across all its
groups; construction standing-reds = 0; kind-isolation excused = 0; the conformance MUST set green for
5 planes; the oracle records every capture-driver cell (wire.*, egress.*, export.* — owner STEP-20)
and replays strict — **RECORDING DONE (ORACLE-CAPTURE, job cli-af9d63d7 on predev c4d725436): 77/77
capture-driver cells recorded natively on predev** (32 wire/egress/export, all in golden/1.5.5). The old
"records none" had one cause: a relative `--bin` spawned nothing. Fixed in busbar-release fe8cc49/a8b59e0;
the routing of every corpus capture cell is pinned by busbar-release #17. Strict replay still has reds,
each routed to its owner: the dropped upstream user-agent (PROTO-FIXES), the streamed gemini grounded cell (PROTO-GEMINI), D-1 inside body.json (oracle
pin bump), and the wire.h2 keepalive-ping capture that is not repeatable (engine); accepted differences re-signed
(DONE, 45/45); the architecture audit per Q128; docs (product docs to 1.6.0), version bump, changelog.
*Exit:* turnstile ADMIT → train ff predev→dev = **DEV-GREEN**.

**Then PERF** (owner joins; frozen DEV-GREEN sha; §11.9), **fixes**, and **DEV-GREEN again**.

**Owner questions open:** none. Q39, Q98, Q99, Q108, Q128, Q129, Q130, Q131 and Q132 were all answered 2026-10-01/02 (`1.6.0-QUESTIONS.md`; spec Law 11, THE DESIGN §5 destination guard). Still unnamed, with no question filed: the two hook behaviour drifts other than HOOK-SYSTEM-TURN, and the unsigned test-fixture re-bless (see the live-defects line below).

**Live defects surfaced by the stock take (no row existed):** two `CallerToken` definitions
(`busbar-contract/src/auth.rs`, `busbar-kernel-identity/src/carrier.rs`); ~~`mint_signed_with_aws`
returns bare `String` secrets~~ (LANDED: e8fbaa327, eb8c61f9f); ~~`refresh_scrape_gauges` → `derived_bucket_usage` reprices on scrape
(violates money-blindness)~~ (LANDED: 434baa868; the scrape reads `viewed_usage_for`); ~~`oracle.pin` and the `mock-upstream` Python path~~ (LANDED: lane ORACLE-PIN-ONE; the one pin is `oracle-rust.pin`, the one far end is the engine's `mock`; guarded by `crates/busbar/tests/oracle_one_pin.rs`); three hook behaviour
drifts (HOOK-SYSTEM-TURN LANDED; the other two are not yet named); an unsigned test-fixture re-bless. Each belongs to the phase that touches its crate.


## THE RULES FOR WORKING THIS LIST

Folded 2026-09-29 from `1.6.0-KICKOFF.md` (the rule book for the coding session) and brought to
today's reality. Every line is binding. Where this section disagrees with a checklist, this section
wins; where it disagrees with a Law in `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` or an owner ruling, those win.

**R0 — Two documents, three records.** The release has exactly two documents:
`docs/design/BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` is THE SPEC (how it is built; authority Part 1 > Part 3 > Part 2 >
Part 5; THE DESIGN, Part 0 §1-§11, is the kernel<>plugins design) and this file is THE WORKLIST (what
is left, who is on it). Three records exist because these rules require them, and none is a
document: `1.6.0-QUESTIONS.md` (the ONLY owner-question queue, rule 1.8), `1.6.0-SLOT-LOG.md` (the
slot reports, rule 15.8) and `1.6.0-PARKED/` (one file per parked money figure, rule 15.4). Read the
spec first, then this file. Do not create another architecture, plan or status document: a binding
decision goes into the spec at the section that governs it, work and status go here.

**Every number in these rules was produced by running the instrument.** A count that cannot be
re-measured is not stated. If you are about to state a count, run the thing that counts it.

### 1. What the session is

**1.1** The session writes code. It does not audit. **1.2** The audit loop is CLOSED by owner ruling;
the filed findings are items 160-571 below. **1.3** Do not open a new audit round and do not spawn a
finder agent; one audit runs over the finished code (rule 17.4). **1.4** 1.3 does not forbid
reading: verifying a claim you are about to act on is required (12.4). What is forbidden is
commissioning a new sweep for defects nobody filed.

**Unattended operation.** **1.7** Never ask the owner anything mid-run — no question tool, no "should
I", no waiting. **1.8** Queue instead: append one row to `1.6.0-QUESTIONS.md` with the slot, the item,
what you could not decide, what you did instead, and what it costs if your choice is wrong. **1.9**
Then carry on: mark the item BLOCKED in your SLOT-LOG row and take the next item. **1.10** Decide when
you can and record it: a choice the code, the spec or an existing ruling settles is not a question.
Queue only what needs the owner's authority — money that changed, a customer-visible behaviour change,
a cost you were not authorised to incur. **1.11** "Stop" means stop THAT ITEM, never the session.
**1.12** The one halt: a phase cannot exit and every remaining slot in it is blocked on one cause —
drain every phase-independent slot first, then stop and write the reason at the top of the questions
file. **1.13** Progress is visible: every finished slot is a SLOT-LOG row; an ETA, when stated, is the
mean wall-clock of finished slots × the slots remaining, and before five have finished it is "—". A
phase is COMPLETE only when its exit test is green, never because its items are ticked (4.4).

**Implementers and the architect.** **1.14** The ARCHITECT (roster id `main`) designs the seams;
agents implement them. An agent never invents a seam: a seam question the spec and the rulings do
not answer goes to `main` with the question and a proposed option, and the agent keeps working on
the rest. **1.15** An agent's report is a lead, not a fact; verify before acting. **1.16** Message only
the ids on the live roster (`main`, the LANDER, the named slots). Messaging a dead id revives a stale
copy that duplicates live work.

### 2. The tree and the train

**2.1** Branch `predev`. Re-read the SHA (`git -C <repo> rev-parse --short origin/predev`); never trust
a SHA written in a document. **2.2** Four branches: `predev`, `dev`, `qa`, `main`; do not create a
fifth. **2.3** `main` is an ancestor of `predev` and the train fast-forwards at every hop. **2.4** `main`
is production; it is never pushed by hand, by anyone. Never push `qa`. **2.5** Branch protection
(owner 2026-09-28): on `qa` only — required CI and train-only pushes; the ARCHITECT drafts the
settings and the OWNER applies them. Re-measure with `gh api repos/GetBusbar/busbar/branches/<b>/protection`
before claiming any protection state.
**2.6 Only LANDER commits to `predev` and pushes it.** Every agent works in its own worktree
(`git -C <repo> worktree add /Users/matthew/Developer/tmp/wt/<slot> -b <branch> origin/predev`, its
`CARGO_TARGET_DIR` inside it) and sends `READY <SLOT>: branch, worktree, shas, base, $|non-$, proof` to
`main` and the LANDER. The LANDER lands non-$ READYs as a TRAIN (stacked, one combined proof, bisect
on red) and every `$` commit alone. A kernel, contract or `$` READY gets a fresh REVIEWER verdict before
it lands. Delete your target directory once your work has landed.
**2.7** `dev` is pushed ONCE, when the release is done, through the turnstile, and the ARCHITECT owns
that call. The owner's sequence (2026-09-29): code 100 % done → the performance phase, run together
with the owner → DEV done. DEV-GREEN is not declared before the performance phase. **2.8**
`turnstile-dispatch.yml` is `workflow_dispatch`-only on purpose; never add `on: push`. Never push `dev`
by hand to route around a denial: a bypassed denial is a deleted gate.

### 3. The work

**3.1** Items 1-159 (and 572+) are the plan's own work, in THE EXECUTION ORDER below; each phase
carries ONE exit test with an exit code. Phase 4's concrete work is the KERNEL<>PLUGINS table.
**3.2** Items 160-571 are the filed audit findings: column four is the defect, column five the
positive control. **3.3** Items marked STRUCK live in files the fold deletes; do not open one.
**3.4** A filed item carries no exit test; you write it when you fix it, and it MUST be able to go
RED (rule 16). **3.5** Raw evidence: `qa/evidence/1.6.0-audit-evidence.json` and
`~/Downloads/busbar-audit-evidence-2026-09-23.tgz`; delete neither.

**Phases.** **4.1** Phases run in order and a phase exits on its exit test. Phases 0, 1, 2 and 3 have
EXITED (SLOT-LOG); Phase 4 is CURRENT; Phase 5 (the RECORD) and PERF (after DEV-GREEN) follow it. A
row added to an exited phase is found-after-exit work, drained in Phase 4 behind that phase's exit
test; a row that turns an exited phase's exit test red is a regression and is fixed first. **4.2** A
slot owns its write set; two slots never edit one file (#27e). A slot that needs a file another slot
owns records the dependency and the ARCHITECT sequences the two (15.1). **4.3** Stream A (the plan's
own items, with the exit test) runs before stream B (the filed backlog) inside a phase, because
stream A names files stream B slots own. **4.4** If every item is green and the exit test is red, the
phase is not done and the items were the wrong items.

### 5. Models

**5.1** Model by the work, not by the slot's importance: **opus** for money, the oracle, anything
carrying a HIGH, kernel mechanism, hook parity, divergence diagnosis and review; **sonnet** for
specified changes, per-plugin fan-out, goldens, renames, tests to a known pattern; **haiku** or no
model at all for running scripts, oracles and gates. **5.2** Money is matched on the defect statement
as well as the path — *billed · invoice · charged · priced · rate-card · spend · token-count ·
metered · overdraft · refund · settles · unpriced* — and the oracle on *oracle · golden ·
byte-identity · openapi*. **5.3** No agent retiers its own slot downward. **5.4** Owner 2026-09-29:
cost-conscious — fresh agents with short handoffs over long-transcript resumes, the cheapest adequate
model, never stop a running agent to save tokens.

### 6. Compute, proof and the Latchkey budget

**6.1 Compute runs only in Latchkey**, through
`/Users/matthew/Developer/tmp/busbar-run/lk-cached.sh <size> '<cmd>'` from your worktree. Never cargo on
the Mac, never EC2 by hand, never a LAN host. The script ships the context itself and sets
`XTASK_CEILING_BASE` to the merge-base with `origin/predev`; a `no-base` gate result in a job means
your branch diverged: rebase. Long output goes to a file you grep; never paste a log.
**6.2 THE PROOF POLICY (owner, 2026-09-29).** An agent proves with ONE Latchkey job, commands
chained with `&&`: (1) `cargo build --workspace --all-targets`; (2) the tests of the crates it touched;
(3) fmt and `clippy -D warnings` on those crates; (4) the gates and oracle families its change can
move (construction and kind-isolation `--posture`, abi-location, qa-names `--posture`,
public-hygiene lint when it touches docs or comments, the oracle families it touches). Not the whole
workspace suite, not every gate. **A `$` change keeps the full money proof on both sides** — agent
and LANDER: `money_one_function_equivalence`, `money-invariants --posture`, `no-float-money`, and
oracle `replay --strict` over `billing|ledger|teller|concurrency` against `golden/1.5.5`. **The
LANDER runs the single full proof per train** — whole workspace tests, every gate, oracle strict,
the integration smoke test — before every push. Nothing lands less proven than before.
**6.3 The budget.** `lk-cached.sh` enforces ONE shared budget: 45 job submissions per rolling hour for
agents, the LANDER (`LK_PRIO=1`) up to 90, and agents are paced to one submission per 60 s (the
LANDER is exempt). When it is full the script waits for a slot on its own, and a submission Latchkey's
quota refuses is un-recorded and retried every 5 minutes, up to 12 times: do not kill it and
resubmit. One proof is one job; never one job per gate or per test; never
resubmit a failed job without reading its log.
**6.4 Dependencies (owner, 2026-09-30: keep bloat in check).** A READY that adds or changes a
third-party dependency carries the `cargo tree` diff — crate names added and their count, for the
default build and for the plugin crate it lands in. One protocol = one library; one crypto backend =
`ring`; a C dependency needs ARCHITECT approval; prefer crates already in `Cargo.lock`. The LANDER
bounces a READY whose `Cargo.lock` grows without that diff.

### 7. Conformance

**7.1** `cargo xtask conformance check --musts` exists and is the reason the turnstile denies; re-run
it for the current count. **7.3** A pass is honoured only if its commit is the release sha, so every
commit to `predev` makes every pass STALE; that is normal in flight, not a regression. **7.4**
Conformance is clean ONCE, on the release sha, in Phase 5. **7.5** The never-run suites are Phase 5
work.

### 8. Money

**8.1** Money is binary: would a bank auditor pass it? If not it is wrong; fix it. **8.2** Do not ask
about money; be right about it. **8.3** Ledger ≠ money: planes write the ledger; money is a view on
the ledger × the rate card. **8.4** The ledger is exactly what the plane did. **8.5** Money is never
wrong; the ledger or the rate card is. **8.6** An unpriced class REFUSES; it never answers zero.
**8.7** Never self-approve a changed money figure (15.4). **8.8** Read-time money counts: a change to
what a customer is charged counts even when no stored byte moved. **8.9** A `$` commit touches money,
lands ALONE, and carries the full money proof (6.2).

### 9. The oracle and the ratchets

**9.1** The oracle is never waived. **9.2** Never bless, re-record or regenerate a golden, an
`accepted-differences.json` entry, an `openapi.json` or a `qa/*.toml` ratchet; recording a golden is
an owner act. **9.3** 9.2 protects the instruments too: weakening, deleting or narrowing a drift test,
a staleness assertion or a comparison is the same offence as blessing what it guards. **9.4** Law 9:
writing a measured ceiling into a `qa/*.toml` for the first time arms an instrument; raising one
relaxes it. **9.5 Owner grant 2026-09-29 — standing ratchet authority, ARCHITECT only**, each use
logged with the measured number in its own commit body, never hidden: (1) a new crate's first measured
cells; (2) an F12 relocation — the same lines move and the total nets down in the same commit;
(3) a kernel or contract line-limit re-arm at the measured value, in its own commit. Any other real
rise is the owner's. **9.6** Customer-visible behaviour: the llm plane and every core surface stay
byte-identical to 1.5.5 unless the owner signs a named change. The four planes new in 1.6.0 (mcp,
a2a, streaming, decisions) have no 1.5.5 behaviour to cite: their baseline for a fold or a move is
current `predev` behaviour, proven by tests, their conformance rigs and the money suites (owner
correction 2026-09-28); never cite 1.5.5 texts for them. **9.7** `ship-ready` (standing kind-isolation
debt == 0) stays RED on `predev` until the plugin program retires the standing debt: it is the cut
gate, the burn-down meter, outside the CI umbrella. Nobody weakens it, excuses a row, or chases it as
a CI fix; the standing count only falls, and a rise is a stop (ARCHITECT ruling 2026-09-27).

### 10. Git

**10.1** Never add `Co-Authored-By` or any AI attribution. **10.2** Always `git -C <path>`; never
`cd <path> && git`. **10.3** Always `git commit --only <paths>`, naming only files you own. **10.4**
The index must be empty before you commit (`git -C <wt> diff --cached --name-only`). **10.5** Never
amend; never force-push. **10.6** Snapshot before any delete, to `~/Downloads/`, and say where in the
commit. **10.7** Never run `git gc`, `git prune` or `git reflog expire` in busbar or busbar-release:
some tags are the only holders of their commit; tag or branch before anything prunes. **10.8** A file
that compiles and is not tracked is invisible to every gate that walks git: `git status --porcelain`
shows no `??` before you report READY.

### 11. Words and safety

**11.1** Banned: *defer* · *1.6.x* · *later* · *"out of scope for 1.6.0"*. **11.2** Speak in kinds,
not instances. **11.3** Secrets are never printed; process argv is never printed. **11.4** Never kill a
process by pattern. **11.5** Settings live in `~/.claude/settings.json`; never a project-level settings
file.

### 12. How you work

**12.1** Max agents, disjoint write sets. **12.2** Run the instrument; do not grep a proxy for it.
**12.3** Root-cause first; agent laziness is fixed at the root, never brought for sign-off. **12.4**
Verify a specific claim before you act on it. **12.5** A gate binary scans the tree it was compiled
in: an `xtask` copied or prebuilt into another worktree scans the wrong tree. Build the gate inside
the tree it judges.

### 13. Gate hygiene

**13.2** `cargo xtask gate --all`'s rollup omits the gates whose red is EXCUSED BY DESIGN — each excuse
is written, dated and states its deletion condition, and the rows still print. Do not "fix" the
rollup. **13.3** Reason about workflows by trigger, not by count: `workflow_call` follows its caller,
`push` the pushed branch, `workflow_run`/`schedule`/`workflow_dispatch` the default branch; workflows
that exist only on `predev` do not block development. Re-derive the set with
`git diff --name-only origin/main origin/predev -- .github/workflows/`.

### 15. What an agent will hit — ruled

**15.1** A slot needs a file another slot owns: do not touch it; record the dependency; the ARCHITECT
sequences. **15.2** An item is a false positive: do not fix it, do not delete it; mark it STRUCK here
with the measurement that refuted it. **15.3** The fix is a deletion: done is the code gone, every
caller gone, and a test asserting the behaviour that replaces it (a refusal, an error, a checked
absence). **15.4** A money figure changed: FIX when you can name the fault (the ledger recorded what
the plane did not do, or the rate card priced a class it should have refused) and write the before
and after in your SLOT-LOG row; PARK when you cannot say which was wrong — `1.6.0-PARKED/<item>.md`
with the cell, the diff, the root cause as far as you got and a recommendation, and that slot does
not land. Either way, a change that reaches an invoice or a served `/usage` figure is queued per 1.8:
the owner is told what moved. **15.5** An exit test lives in the crate it tests, in that directory's
existing test module; a bare script wires its own `--selftest` and CI calls it; never a new top-level
test crate. **15.6** A newly created file belongs to the slot that created it; say so in the report.
**15.7** Two slots' commits conflict: the partition is wrong — stop that pair, re-cut the write sets,
never resolve by hand. **15.8** A slot reports by appending ONE row to `1.6.0-SLOT-LOG.md` (slot,
items, verdict per item FIXED / STRUCK / PARKED / BLOCKED, the exit test path, the RED proof) and a
READY of at most fifteen lines (shas, proof, anything owner-pending). **15.9** A phase that cannot
exit is the 1.12 halt. **15.10** Check WHAT THE OWNER STILL OWES (below) before declaring anything
blocked.

### 16. Proving a test goes RED

**16.1** Every behaviour change ships a test that fails without it. **16.2** Prove it in your own
worktree: the test first, run against the unfixed tree in a Latchkey job, capture the non-zero exit,
then the fix; record the command and its exit in your SLOT-LOG row. **16.3** Prove RED by reading when
reading suffices; a build costs budget.

### 17. What closes the release

**17.1** `scripts/verify-1.6.0-done.sh`, FULL run — never `--fast` (exit 3 is PROVISIONAL). **17.2**
Every bless/repoint environment variable empty. **17.3** Conformance clean on the release sha (7.4).
**17.4** Then, and only then, the architecture audit over the finished code (Q128). **17.5** Then DEV-GREEN,
then PERF with the owner on the frozen sha, then the fixes, then DEV-GREEN again (OWNER 2026-09-30).

**STATUS 2026-09-29 (`origin/predev` `23d8a6b46`): Phases 0-3 EXITED; Phase 4 CURRENT, its concrete
plan the KERNEL<>PLUGINS table (measured status in each row); Phase 5 and PERF (after DEV-GREEN) (paused until the
owner joins) follow. The done-oracle read 1 of 23 groups green at `b461d61b1`; a reclassification of
its groups is running.** (Origin: 157 items — 109 from the sweep (1-111, two struck), 41 from the code
audit (112-156), 7 lettered sub-items — plus the filed backlog 160-571 and items 572+.)

**Two of the four claims are REFUTED by measurement, not by argument.** C3 diverges on 16 cells
where its own criterion is one; C2 fails for the `export` kind, which cannot be dropped in at all.
C1 is unmeasured over five of its seven kinds. C4's instruments cannot currently produce a NO.
The measured status of each is in `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Part 0.

When every item here is done, the four claims in `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Part 0 return YES and the train
moves. Nothing else counts. Ordered by **dependency**, not by date — owner ruling 10: *"forget a
date, i want a plan for DONE."*

Every figure was measured, not estimated, and re-verified by the architect before landing here.
The sweep's own coverage proof FAILED once and the failure is recorded in `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Law 8:
ten slices were assigned by path prefix, `busbar-k` < `busbar-kernel` < `busbar-l`, and the nine
kernel crates — 574 files of CORE — fell in neither range. An eleventh slice took them, and items
99-111 are what it found. Proving every file has an owner is not proving the owners' scopes tile.

> **Why the order is what it is.** Waves 0 and 1 come first because without them every later wave
> is unmeasurable or unshippable. A gate that cannot fail turns a green board into no evidence at
> all, and a switched-off release engine means a 100%-green tree still never leaves the shed. The
> sweep found both conditions live.

## HOW TO READ THIS DOCUMENT

**If you are going to WORK an item, you need two sections and nothing else:** THE EXECUTION ORDER
immediately below (what runs when, and the exit test that says a phase is done), and the item
tables (1-111 from the sweep, 112-156 from the audit). Everything after those is EVIDENCE — the
measurements and refutations each item rests on. Read it when an item surprises you, not before.

**Nothing here is estimated.** Every figure was measured and re-verified by the architect. Where a
figure was wrong it was corrected in place and the correction says so, because a plan that quietly
fixes its own numbers cannot be audited either.

---

## THE MEASUREMENT HAZARD IN THIS CHECKOUT — read before running anything

`predev` moved **four times** under a running measurement during this session, and the agents doing
the measuring noticed before I did. One consequence already landed: the audit-ledger deletion went
in 10.5 minutes into a workspace test run, so that run built a binary from before the deletion and
read a `ci.yml` from after it, and left three tests reading a file that no longer existed.

**The rule that follows:** any measurement that takes longer than a commit runs against a **pinned
worktree**, never against `predev`. `git worktree add --detach <pin> HEAD`, and put the pin outside
`/private/tmp` — the hourly reaper sweeps that directory and ate an audit pin mid-run today,
truncating six of ten finders, which could not tell "found nothing" from "tree gone". The reaper now
skips any directory carrying a `.codeaudit-pin` marker, but the placement rule is the real fix.

Also live in this checkout: two `xtask` files sit **staged** in the index that this session did not
stage. A bare `git commit` here sweeps them in. Every commit in this session used
`git commit --only <paths>` for that reason, and so should every commit after it.

# BACKLOG BY ORIGIN PHASE (the ORDER is PATH TO DEV-GREEN above; these phases are where the items came from)

The seven waves below this section are the **inventory** — what is owed, grouped by claim. This
section is the **order**, grouped by what must be TRUE before the next group is judgeable. They are
different questions and the plan previously conflated them, which is where the cycle came from.

**The rule that governs every phase boundary: a phase is done when its EXIT TEST passes, not when
its items are ticked.** An exit test is one command with an exit code. If a phase's items are all
green and its exit test is red, the phase is not done and the items were the wrong items.

### THE C1 HOLE, stated plainly because it is the release's central claim
C1 says *"core names no instance."* It is asserted over **7 plugin kinds** and measured over **2**.
`kind_isolation/matrix.rs:116` is `INSTANCE_VOCAB_KINDS = &["plane", "transport"]`, and `:1193`
skips every other kind. The counter-example is in core and I verified it by hand:
`busbar-kernel/src/config/sections.rs:435` defines `EXPORT_MODULES` — **a closed list of export
plugin INSTANCE names inside the engine** — and `config/mod.rs:1983` refuses any name outside it.

That is not only a C1 breach. It is a **C2 breach**: a dropped-in export plugin cannot be named in
a config file at all, so for the `export` kind the compiled-in fold and the dropped-in fold are not
the same contract. One hole, two claims, no instrument that can see it.

## KERNEL<>PLUGINS — the migration to the final design (ARCHITECT 2026-09-27)

**Where it sits.** This is the C1/C2 work of Phase 4 made concrete: the kernel speaks kinds and
never loads, and every plugin has one row, one Statement and one door. It runs beside Phase 4's
drains and must exit before Phase 5 reads the tree. The design is `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` (the
kernel<>plugins rows being folded in now, cited here by topic); this table is the ORDER.

### THE PLUGIN ABI LOCK — owner, 2026-09-27 (BUSBAR-1.6.0 THE DESIGN §11)

Plugins speak the memory ABI only; seven kind ABIs share one mechanism; compiled-in = dropped-in
through the same table; JSON only as a payload; every ABI shape in `busbar-contract/src/abi/`; auth
and hooks on the memory ABI; no legacy loading; performance is PHASE SIX (below). The owner's
execution model: *"kernel has 1 way to load plugins, find out its kind, and use it as needed. we just
design the kind abis and spin up agents to implement for every plugin parallel"* — *"5 planes, 5
agents implementing the same ABI interface, siblings"*. The work runs in three stages, in this order:

**(a) KIND ABI DESIGN** — running: a design pass and an adversarial review of the seven kind ABIs
(store, secret, auth, hook, export, plane, transport) on the shared mechanism, each kind's fixed
fields vs payload blobs decided once. The architect writes its result in here.

| # | item | status |
|---|---|---|
| ABI-a | the seven kind ABIs + the shared mechanism, designed and adversarially reviewed | **DONE** — owner-signed as THE DESIGN §11.11 (2026-09-27); the shapes landed as M0/M3 (below) |

**(b) KERNEL SIDE** — built once, before the fan-out:

| # | item | status |
|---|---|---|
| ABI-b1 | ONE loader, ONE kind lookup, ONE dispatcher — the only way the kernel loads, identifies and calls a plugin of any kind | **IN-FLIGHT (BOOT-CHAIN for the loader and kind lookup)** — the dispatcher landed as M1 (b51a83402..59611e862) and is built once at boot 8ed0d5019, held but unread (unwired seam H6) |
| ABI-b2 | delete every compiled-in Rust shortcut — the six kinds whose compiled-in plugins bypass the ABI today (measured 2026-09-27): **store, hook, secret, auth, transport, plane**. After it the kernel holds no plugin crate's Rust types and calls no plugin's Rust functions; export already goes through the ABI | **IN-FLIGHT (WIRE-STORE, WIRE-HOOK, WIRE-AUTH, WIRE-SECRET, WIRE-EXPORT, K1 for planes, CONNECTOR-19 for transports)** |
| ABI-b3 | the SDK (`busbar-contract/src/abi/sdk/`, typed wrappers) + the generated C header (#84's zero-dependency proof) | **DONE** — the door macro a03e3d1eb, the safe surface c25559853/8b6ab663a, the generated header `busbar_plugin.h`; #84's zero-dependency proof is the plugin repo GetBusbar/busbar-secret-c (C, from the header alone, no busbar import), tested by itself through plugin-ci.yml's C path (`dropped_conformance` via `load_dropped`, RED arm at `kind_abi ± 1`) and registered in plugins.yaml (`lang: c`). LEDGER R55 closed by that repo. The separate Rust `busbar-hook-zerodep` witness is struck as redundant: every Rust fleet plugin proves the Rust path |

**(c) FAN-OUT** — one agent per plugin, siblings per kind, each implementing its kind's ABI; the
kind's conformance suite (ABI-b4) is the finish line for every sibling:

| kind | siblings (one agent each) |
|---|---|
| plane | llm, mcp, a2a, streaming, decisions |
| auth | outbound styles, admin-tokens, oidc, github, ldap |
| store | memory, postgres, mysql, sqlite, valkey |
| secret | env, file, vault |
| hook | ranking, webrequest (headroom removed, OWNER 2026-09-30) |
| export | prometheus, otlp, webhook, file |
| transport | tcp, stdio, http, ws, unix |

**(d) ROUND 2 REVIEW — owner-signed 2026-09-27 (BUSBAR-1.6.0 THE DESIGN §11.11).** A two-round
adversarial review (hot path, parity, contract lenses) read the ABI-a design against `b48a79a9a` and
against v1.5.5 and disagreed on nine points; the owner ruled on all nine. Full text: THE DESIGN §11.11.

| ruling | decision |
|---|---|
| R1 Hooks (Q-LEAK) | every hook call runs on an off-worker lane with 1.5.5's `timeout_ms` guarantee (not only `cpu_heavy`, no per-plugin flag); a crossing watchdog plus worker replacement contains a wedged call; `dlopen_decide_deadline_cuts_off_a_slow_gate` and `dlopen_slow_gate_hits_the_deadline` pass as written; cost measured in PHASE SIX |
| R2 Quarantine | exit rule = a timed probe, circuit-breaker style (backoff, one trial call); registered as an accepted difference from 1.5.5 |
| R3 Q-INCACHE | the inbound credential cache moves into the auth plugins (SDK `VerifyCache`, flushed through `refresh`); the kernel keeps only Pass buffering |
| R4 Q-DISK | a host-owned bounded disk lane is the one exception to "no blocking", scoped to exactly the SQLite store and the file export sink |
| R5 Q-MYSQL | sans-IO mysql store and ldap auth are built; both ship |
| R6 oauth2/admin | stay core, not plugins; the door-only gate exempts exactly their two `OnceLock` seams and refuses any new one |
| R7 Codec fold | `busbar-llm-codec`/`busbar-voice-codec` fold into their plane crates; kernel and binary never depend on a codec crate |
| R8 Accepted differences | Q-BODY REJECTED (hooks see the 1.5.5 view exactly); 503 on auth-verify overload ACCEPTED; Q-EXPIRED only if measurement shows a real 1.5.5 difference |
| R9 Structs/versions | extensions-first; a fixed-field append is a version bump; `TRANSPORT_VERSION` renamed `MECHANISM_VERSION` = 2; `ABI_MAJOR`/`ABI_MINOR`/`POD_VERSION`/`TRANSPORT_DECL_MAJOR` retired |

**Implementation requirements from the review — ARCHITECT-owned, each with its fix (THE DESIGN §11.11
carries the full table):**

| # | requirement | fix |
|---|---|---|
| H2 | tickets have no generation | `(slot, generation)` tickets, latched, spurious-tolerant |
| H3 | tap JSON rebuilt per request | tap pool holds the fixed stage view; plugin builds its own JSON, off-path |
| H4 | unbounded never-drop write_behind queue | coalesced batches, one `op_id` each, durable dedupe retention |
| H5 | write_behind to a hung store stalls reload drain | its own deadline class, reload drain does not wait on it |
| H6 | continuation/ticket ownership across a hook chain undefined | host-owned for the chain's lifetime, sized and documented per kind |
| M2/contract | kernel names protocols (`jsonrpc-ingress`, `card-signing`, `relay`, `duplex-ws`, `egress-*`, `dispatch`, `openapi-schema`) | delete the nine features; widen `c1-literals` to all 7 kinds + feature names + `cfg` sites |
| M3/contract | `extern "C-unwind"` across cdylib is UB | `extern "C"`, abort on escape |
| M4/contract | linked-plugin tracing/log reaches kernel subscriber, dropped-in doesn't; std thread/net/fs/env undenied | `plugin-closure-deps` denies `tracing`, `log`, `std::thread`, `std::net`, `std::fs`, `std::env` |
| M5/contract | cargo feature unification differs linked vs dropped | a feature-set-equality gate between the two builds |
| M6/contract | both-ways suite tests the test profile, "counters above 0" too weak | runs against the release binary, exact crossing counts ~~(suite in busbar)~~ (the published suite, run in each plugin repo's CI; OWNER 2026-10-03 (no test plugins)) |
| M7/contract | "append under `honoured_size`" conflicts with extensions-first; FAULT-prefill vs zeroed `out` contradiction | resolved by R9; one stated rule: host pre-fills `out` |
| M8/contract | store `reserve`/`slice_release`/record ops carry JSON unit maps on the request path | fixed `bb_units[]` |
| M9/contract | inbound cache adopted before Q-INCACHE answered | resolved by R3 |
| M10/contract | plane crates depend on codec crates, conflicting with contract-only deps | resolved by R7 |
| M2/parity | quarantine has no exit rule, unregistered | resolved by R2 |
| M3/parity (money) | draft bills a translate-abort "unless failed" | pin cancel billing to the four 1.5.5 rules; budget refund is a separate act |
| M4/parity | http rewrite has no 1.5.5 wire-byte oracle | step 20 re-scoped, see below |
| M5/parity | rewritten export/store/secret plugins have no golden vs 1.5.5; `op_id` dedupe durability unstated | golden 1.5.5 suite per export stream and store backend; durable dedupe (ties H4). **Each store sibling's `migrate()` must upgrade its own 1.5.x usage-ledger rows (scalar `tokens` onto `usage_units`); the per-backend 1.5.5 golden is the proof** (#33, ARCHITECT 2026-09-29: the engine holds no fold, and the ABI<4 legacy adapter retires at M6) |
| M6/parity | hook view's budget/cost fields unlisted | every field listed, marked static or dynamic |
| M7/parity | 503-on-overload and unbounded queue unregistered | 503 registered under R8; queue bounded per H4 |

no-new-static-seam check, R6), and a linked/dropped feature-set-equality gate (M5/contract).

**Execution policy — models are chosen per work type, not by default:**
- **Opus:** kernel mechanism, money, hook parity, divergence diagnosis, review.
- **Sonnet:** per-plugin fan-out, landing, goldens, renames, mechanical moves.
- **Haiku, or no model at all:** running scripts, oracles and gates. An oracle or gate run is a
  background shell command that produces a verdict file — it is not reasoning work.

and 36 for PHASE SIX; streams-2000 stays at step 23 as a correctness check.

## THE DENOMINATOR, MEASURED — every LOC figure above this line was fiction

`wc -l` over `crates/` and `xtask/` answers **870,458**. That number is not a measurement of
anything: it counts doc comments (this tree writes a great many), blank lines, and test code — and
test code here is **57% of all Rust in the repo**. `cargo xtask loc` is a real lexer (nested
block comments, strings containing `//`, raw strings), proven both directions before use.

| | lines of CODE |
|---|---|
| the product (`crates/` less xtask) | **189,075** |
| the instruments (`xtask/`) | **69,308** |
| production code, total | **258,383** |
| test code | **337,086** — 1.8× the product |

**The instruments are 27% the size of the product they measure.** Consequences for this plan:

- `busbar-kernel` is **50,694** lines of code, not 186,671.
- `busbar-llm-codec` is **22,063**, not 105,589 — item 59's move is a fifth of its recorded size.
- Every "N thousand lines" estimate in the fold plan and in items 59/74/75/81 must be re-derived.

## TWO BREAKERS SHIP, AND THE BUGGY ONE IS THE LIVE ONE

The concurrency lens reported three races in `store/in_memory/breaker.rs` and noted each is
"already fixed in its `busbar-kernel-breaker` twin." I checked which twin is wired, and the
reassurance inverts:

| | code lines | reached from |
|---|---|---|
| `store/in_memory/breaker.rs` — **has the three races** | 578 | the kernel's own admission path (`vocab.rs:493-553`, `availability.rs:6`) |
| `busbar-kernel-breaker` — **has the fixes** | 1,198 | **one** production file, `busbar/src/root/adapters.rs`; everything else is tests |

Positive control: `busbar_kernel_ledger` is referenced from 34 crates under the identical search;
`busbar_kernel_breaker` from five files, four of them tests.

**So the corrected logic is the one that is nearly unreached, and the defective logic is the one
deciding admissions.** The three races are live:

1. `breaker.rs:400` — `breaker_verdict` loads `cooldown_until` **before** `breaker_state`, the same
   order every writer writes them. `cell.rs:246-252` documents that order as *"an obligation, not a
   style choice."* A concurrent hard-down makes a benched lane report `usable: true` on `/stats`
   and `/healthz`; the mirror case drops a just-recovered lane from the healthy set and sheds the
   request.
2. `breaker.rs:795` — the under-lock re-check refuses unless `state == ST_OPEN`, so a cell a peer's
   probe just **closed** — ready to serve — is Denied instead of `ReadyNoProbe`.
3. `availability.rs:355` — `ProbeAdmit::Denied` carries no reason, so a cooling denial is reported
   as `ProbeInFlight`, whose `recovery_hint_ms` is **250 ms** against a real cooldown of up to
   **1,800,000 ms**. The twin splits `DeniedBy::Cooling { until }` from `DeniedBy::ProbeInFlight`
   for exactly this.

**This is the fold argument in miniature, and it belongs to the owner's milestone.** Two
implementations of one concept both ship; the duplication is not stylistic, it is a live
correctness difference, and no instrument compares them. Folding these two is worth more than
folding any two crates chosen to hit a count.

---

## A DOUBLE-BILLING CLAIM, REFUTED BY READING THE CODE — and what is really there

A lens reported that the live LLM path posts the same request's usage to **two independent money
ledgers**: the synchronous `ledger_and_meter` inside `FirstByteBody` (`response_body.rs:869,:984`)
and an asynchronous late accrual via `Walk::reported_after_terminal` (`walk.rs:474`) reading the
identical `TapCell`. It flagged its own uncertainty, correctly, because resolving it needed code
outside its slice.

**Double-charging is the worst defect available here, so I read it rather than escalate it.
It is not happening.** `walk.rs:464` states the design in terms that leave no room:

> *"It ACCRUES NOTHING. The tap already put this response on the governance ledger when it filled
> the cell, which is what the previous release bills and what `/usage` reports; calling the accrual
> seam again here would bill the same tokens twice. What this produces is a reading, for a second
> book to post onto."*

And `teller.rs:1222`'s `settle_late` is not a second write either — it is the `Err` arm of a match
on `Posted::into_parent`, i.e. the fallback for a child whose parent exited first. **Exactly one
branch fires.** The comment beside it: *"one posting, one sealed end… always posted, flagged as
late, against a synchronous draw."*

**What IS there, and it is the C4 question.** The system keeps **two books** over one event: the
governance ledger the tap fills (what 1.5.5 bills, what `/usage` reports) and the new kernel-loop
book the reading posts onto. One charge, two records — which is sound only while the two agree.
The oracle measures that they do not: `billing|rate-card|history-mid-window` reports different
figures per surface, across `by_key`, `by_model` and `total`.

So the item is not "stop double-billing." It is: **name which book is the invoice, prove the other
agrees with it cell by cell, and make a disagreement RED.** That is C4's own text — *"money is a
view on the ledger"* — applied to the case where there are two ledgers. It belongs beside the
money-authority flip, because the second book arrived with it.

*Method note, since it nearly cost a false escalation:* the reporting lens was honest about its
boundary and said so. The doc that settled it was two screens above the line it quoted. **Read the
module, not the call site** — that is the positive-control rule applied to intent rather than to
existence.

---

## THE CODEC: A TOKEN COUNT THAT CANNOT BE READ IS BILLED AS ZERO

Two HIGH findings, both "silently free", both on the revenue plane.

**1. `anthropic/reader.rs:732`, and the identical pattern in ALL SIX dialects, buffered and
streaming.** Primary chat-completion usage extraction is `read_count_u64(..).unwrap_or(0)`. A
present-but-unreadable token count — a stringified `"1500"` from a compatible self-hosted
backend — **bills as $0 instead of refusing.**

The damning part: **the correct function already exists in the same crate.** `usage_count::billed_count`
refuses exactly this input, and it is used for the transcription and image leaf ops — but never for
the flagship chat path. Verified live: `cargo test -p busbar-llm-codec usage_count` proves
`read_count_u64` genuinely rejects such input, so the `unwrap_or(0)` is converting a refusal into a
free request.

This is decision #42's rule — *a hit class not priced ⇒ REFUSE, never a silent 0* — violated at the
very first step, before any gate or ledger sees the number.

**2. `ir/rerank.rs:97`.** `RerankResp.search_units` is correctly read off Cohere's and Bedrock's
wire and then **discarded**: `RerankResp::billing()` hardcodes `Billing::Flat`. A 5,000-document
rerank bills identically to a 1-document one. The comment defers it to *"the 1.3 pricing engine"*;
the tree is at 1.6.0.

**3. MEDIUM — `anthropic/mod.rs:567`.** The streaming block-index DoS clamp
(`MAX_ANTHROPIC_BLOCK_INDEX = 1023`) collides two distinct out-of-range tool-call indices into one;
the Gemini egress writer reads a repeated index as "block re-opening", clears the accumulator, and
so corrupts one tool call's arguments and drops the other entirely. It needs indices ≥1024 from a
non-conforming upstream — the same threat model the clamp itself exists to defend against.

**Refuted by the same lens, worth recording:** `lib.rs:163`'s "the order is load-bearing" claim is
true for the boot-time protocol list, but the telemetry half of the comment overstates it — build
and lookup both re-derive position from one call in one process, and Prometheus series carry the
protocol name as an explicit label, so a reorder cannot desync telemetry.

---

## THE CONVERGENCE: ONLY FOUR TOKEN COUNTERS ARE EVER MONEY, AT EVERY LAYER

Four lenses hit this from four directions without knowing about each other. Stated as one finding,
because it is one.

busbar's billing model says a plane reports a name-keyed `Usage` map and money is a view over it.
In the shipped binary **only the four reserved token keys (`input`, `output`, `cache_read`,
`cache_write`) can ever become money.** Every other declared class — `bytes`, `tool_calls`,
`audio_seconds_in`, `audio_tokens_in/out`, `text_tokens_in/out`, `cached_tokens`, `decision`, a2a
`hops`, mcp `calls` — is dropped, and it is dropped **four separate times**:

| layer | mechanism | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **the ABI** | the minor-20 keyed-unit tail (`units_ptr`/`units_len`) — **the field ABI_MINOR 19→20 and POD_VERSION 2→3 were bumped to carry** — is decoded by nothing outside its own crate's unit tests. The host never reads it | `busbar-plugin/src/hot/pod.rs:1008` |
| **the host meter** | the hot ABI promises identical metering for all four `UsageComponent`s; the only host implementation records units for **`Tokens` alone**. `Bytes`/`Frames`/`Queries` land in the ledger as a request with every unit counter zero | `pod.rs:272` |
| **the budget gate** | sums only `RESERVED_UNITS`; `RateNanos::reserved_rate` answers `_ => 0` for everything else, while `accrue`, `total_tokens`, `flush_budgets` and `hydrate_budgets` on the same cell are all open-keyed | `records.rs:601`, `cost.rs:175` |
| **the config grammar** | `RateEntryCfg` is `deny_unknown_fields` with exactly four `_utok` floats, so **no open class can be priced from config at all** | — |

**So the two planes that meter in open classes cannot be billed or capped.** MCP and A2A never
accrue anything into the budget ledger — `grep -rn -e meter_ledger -e record_usage` across both
crates returns **nothing**, against 9 files for `busbar-llm`/`busbar-voice`. They meter
`UsageComponent::Queries` at amount **0**, which `token_usage_for` maps to `None`. With
`per_request_fee` defaulting to 0, **a `budget:` cap is unreachable by those planes entirely** —
while A2A's refusal text tells the caller *"this key's budget is spent."*

**Why this is one finding and not four.** Fixing any single layer changes nothing: priced open
classes in config still hit a gate that sums four keys; a widened gate still reads units the host
never recorded; a fixed host still reads an ABI tail nothing decodes. It has to be closed end to
end or not started. That makes it the largest coherent piece of work in Phase 2 and the one most
likely to be mis-scoped as four small items.

**And it explains the shape of the whole money section.** The reserved four are the only units that
work, which is why every defect found lands on planes or paths that use anything else: a2a hops
charged before refusal, mcp queries metered at zero, streaming audio-ms discarded on barge-in,
audio-seconds unpriceable. The design is name-keyed; the implementation is four hardcoded columns.

---

## THE PATTERN WITH A NAME: TWO IMPLEMENTATIONS OF ONE THING, BOTH SHIPPING

Found twice, independently, by lenses that were not looking for it. This is the strongest argument
for the owner's crate fold that the audit produced, and it is stronger than any count.

| concept | the two implementations | which one is live | the consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **circuit breaker** | `store/in_memory/breaker.rs` (578 code lines) vs `busbar-kernel-breaker` (1,198) | the **578-line one**, on the kernel's admission path. The 1,198-line one is reached from a single production file | three live races — a benched lane reports `usable: true`, a just-closed cell is Denied, and a cooling denial advertises a 250 ms recovery hint against a real cooldown of up to 1,800,000 ms. **All three are already fixed in the copy nothing calls** |
| **realtime voice** | the old `busbar-voice` plane vs the new `StreamingPlane` (`claims.rs:150`) | **both** — compiled in and registered by default | two independent, non-communicating metering pipelines over one capability: a D2 nanodollar lease and a meter-class rate card. This is the "two rosters disagreeing" consequence of the incomplete voice→streaming rename, in money |

A third instance is the money-authority flip above: the kernel loop and the substrate loop were two
implementations, and the resolution was to delete one while four files still describe it as live.

**The rule this yields.** A fold is not justified by reaching a crate count. It is justified when
two implementations of one concept both ship and **no instrument compares them** — because then the
duplication is not redundancy, it is an unmeasured behavioural difference. Both cases above are
exactly that, and both are money. Fold these two first, ahead of any crate chosen to hit 35.

---

## STREAMING: THREE WAYS AUDIO IS SERVED AND NOT BILLED

The streaming plane bills by elapsed time, which makes the session lifecycle a money path.

- `busbar-plane-streaming/src/plane.rs:1038` — a Twilio call ending mid-turn (`stop`) **discards the
  open turn's accumulated audio-ms and tool-call counters** instead of billing them.
- `plane.rs:1130` — a client-authored **barge-in does the same**, with no compensating `Close`
  event, on every OpenAI-Realtime / Gemini-Live session supporting client-side interrupt.
- `busbar-voice/src/mount.rs:1544` (also `telephony.rs:209`, `mount.rs:1572`) — **every production
  call site discards the durable `SessionHandle` without ever calling `settle_terminal()` or
  `close()`**, and the plane's own idle-abandon callback (`scope.rs:159`) opts OUT of the sweep. So
  every voice session's durable row leaks in memory forever.

That third one is the abandoned-hold defect again, on a different plane, by a different mechanism —
the fourth independent route to the same conclusion: **the settle/sweep half of the money protocol
was designed and never wired.**

Plus `runtime/carrier.rs:87` — unbounded mpsc channels on the downlink/uplink audio relay, so a
stalled session grows memory without bound and nothing detects the stall.

---

## PANICS: THERE IS NO PANIC-CATCHING LAYER, AND ONE ADMIN PATCH CAN KILL A SHARD'S EGRESS

The opus escalation read **39 of 65 files line by line** (16,460 LOC) — the whole non-test request
path — after the cheap model returned zero over a fraction of it. It found the slice genuinely
hardened (50 `unwrap`/`expect` sites, each traced to a caller; poison-recovering locks nearly
everywhere; no runtime division in the slice) and produced one determination that matters more than
its three findings.

**There is no panic-catching layer anywhere in the workspace.** `CatchPanic`/`catch_panic` → **0
matches**; `tower-http` is not a dependency. Positive control: the router carries 10 `.layer(`
calls, so the search path is right. The 138 production `catch_unwind` sites are at the plugin ABI
boundary, not on the request path. With `panic = "unwind"` kept deliberately, **a panic on the
request path does not return 500 — it unwinds the hyper connection task, so the client sees a torn
down TCP connection and every request multiplexed on that h2 connection dies with it.** The process
survives. That is the actual blast radius of every `unwrap` in the tree, and it is not what a
reader would assume.

**Two agents disagreed about `pool_idle_timeout_secs`; I checked, and the alarming reading is
right.** The concurrency lens judged the overflow unreachable because the timeout "is not
operator-settable." It is: `config/limits.rs:244` declares it `pub pool_idle_timeout_secs: u64`
with `#[serde(default)]`, and `busbar-core-admin/.../handlers.rs:2785` exposes
`"limits.pool_idle_timeout_secs"` on the admin PATCH surface.

So: an admin PATCH setting it at or above 2⁶³ makes `egress/engine/pool.rs:793` overflow
`Instant + Duration` **while holding the pool mutex**; std poisons the mutex; and all nine
`.expect("engine pool lock")` sites — including `checkout` on the hot path — panic on every
subsequent request. **That shard's egress is dead until restart.** The egress engine and
`byte_duplex` are the only two subsystems in the tree that cascade a poisoned lock rather than
recovering it.

Two smaller ones worth keeping: `ingress/byte_duplex.rs:467` leaks an `inflight` key forever if a
plane callback panics (its sibling at `plane/handle_engine.rs:858` wraps the equivalent callback in
`catch_unwind` for exactly this reason), and `plane/cost.rs:221` checks its one money invariant with
the **raw non-saturating** `u128` `Sum`, bypassing the saturating `Sum` the same module defines 160
lines earlier explicitly against "a hostile/buggy plugin breakdown" — so release wraps and can
validate an ill-formed breakdown.

# THE CONTRACT CRATE: 7 HIGH DOC-LIES — AND ONE I HAD TO CUT DOWN BEFORE PASSING IT ON

The contract lens read **125 of 125 files (38,410 LOC)** — full coverage of the slice round 2 left
unread — and **ran** the gates rather than inferring them. 10 findings, 7 HIGH. This is the crate
named `contract`; every other crate trusts it.

## FIRST, THE CORRECTION, because it was nearly an escalation

The lens's headline was that *"only the kernel mints tokens, CI-enforced"* is false, citing
**"135/264/48/5 real non-kernel call sites"**. I ran the gate. It is genuinely RED:

```
FAIL  seal-witness:single-minter   a non-kernel site obtains the kernel seal
      135 site(s) of `KernelSeal::acquire_for_kernel(` outside crates/busbar-kernel/src/
```

Then I asked the question the word *"real"* was covering. Of **139** sites outside the kernel:

- **135 are test files or `src/tests/` modules**
- **the other 4 are DOC-COMMENT EXAMPLES** — `//! let seal = KernelSeal::acquire_for_kernel();` in
  `caps/{hold,token,decision,unit_end}.rs`
- **PRODUCTION SITES: ZERO**

**The token-minting invariant is intact in production.** Reporting "135 real non-kernel sites can
mint a kernel seal" would have been materially false in the direction that causes panic.

**But the finding survives, transformed, and is arguably worse as an instrument defect:**

1. A **BLOCKING** CI step (`ci.yml:574`) is permanently RED on **test-fixture debt**, none of it on
   the standing-reds allowlist. That is the permanently-red-gate disease this plan already names —
   it trains everyone to read this red as "that one always fails."
2. **The gate counts a NAME, not a THING.** Four of its hits are doc comments. It greps the literal
   `KernelSeal::acquire_for_kernel(` with no idea whether the line is code. That is exactly the
   `design_bindings/tables.rs:602` problem — 409 prose strings poisoning a census — reappearing
   inside a gate whose subject is the release's tightest security invariant.

**The rule this re-proves, now from the other side:** *a name is not a thing* cuts both ways. It has
cost me six false positives by finding a name where the thing was absent; here it nearly cost a
false positive by finding 135 names where the thing was harmless.

## The other six HIGH — all verified by the lens running the code, not reading it

| where | the claim, and what is true |
|---|---|
| `caps/egress.rs:138` | `SecretOnce`'s *"exactly once or the mint is reversed"* has **zero implementing code anywhere**; its "verbs unit only" claim is separately gate-confirmed RED |
| `dest.rs:355` | a **second, dead, differently-shaped `SecretOnce`/`AuthDecoration` pair** exported from the crate root, zero production callers, doc-mismatched to its own constructor. **Fourth sighting of two-implementations-both-shipping** |
| `caps/unit_end.rs:211` | *"only the exit path can build one"* — contradicted by `busbar-kernel/src/tick.rs:322`, an independent second builder. **`tick.rs` is the same file that was the invisible third caller in round 2's `HoldCell::take` finding** |
| `plane.rs:53` | cites an **"interior-mutability scan" gate that does not exist** anywhere in xtask's ~58-entry registry |
| `records.rs:503` | `CredentialMeta::slot`'s documented **SigV4 overlap-window rotation has no implementing code**; the one rotate endpoint explicitly does not touch SigV4 credentials |
| `tests/feature_invariance.rs:17` | *"exactly two feature gates"* — `test-seal` is a third, and **the enforcing test actually fails when run** (`4 passed; 2 failed`). Independent confirmation of item 112 from a second direction |

Plus three MEDIUM: the crate's own canonical *"what Rust actually enforces"* table cites **dead type
names**; *"exactly `IDEMPOTENCY_TTL_SECS`"* is **two independently-defined, coincidentally-equal
constants**; and the *"single JSON seam"* is bypassed by two production `.to_string()` calls
**invisible to its own guarding self-test** (wrong pattern, wrong scope).

**`tick.rs` has now been the hidden second or third writer in three separate findings** — the hold
cell's take, the unit-end builder, and the sweep. It is worth reading in full as its own item
rather than meeting it three more times.

# A2A: 21 FINDINGS, 7 HIGH — AND A BREAKER WITH NO IMPLEMENTATION

The a2a lens re-read all 51 non-test source files (25,595 LOC) across six overlapping passes, each
cross-verifying the others. **21 findings, 7 HIGH**, none overlapping round 2's.

**Verified by me:**

- **`registry.rs:135` — the anomaly circuit breaker can never trip.** Its observation window is
  never written in production: **`impl Window` appears 0 times**, against a positive control of 3
  `impl` blocks in the same file. So the breaker — **including its egress-spend signal** — is a
  structure that is read and never fed. There is also no config surface for its thresholds. This is
  the instrument-that-cannot-fire shape, sitting on a money signal.
- **`originate.rs:164` — busbar's OWN outbound hops are unmetered, unaudited, and exempt from the
  breaker.** The line is literally `breakers: None,`. These are list-refresh and push-config
  mirroring hops: they **dial real backends with a leased credential**, spend real money, and are
  recorded nowhere. It is the exact mirror of round 2's `receive.rs:2085` — that one bills a hop
  that never happened; this one performs a hop that is never billed.

**Reported with the lens's evidence, not independently re-verified by me** (my own line probe landed
on unrelated content, so I am not claiming it):

- **`plane.rs:240` — any admin config apply, even one unrelated to A2A, rebuilds the whole agent
  registry from the fail-closed floor**, wiping approval, pin and cached card for **every** agent
  until a human re-approves each one by hand. If that holds, an unrelated config change is an
  outage requiring manual per-agent recovery.
- `receive.rs:607` — the REST `GET /a2a/agents/{id}` handler runs admission and signing but **never
  meters or audits**, unlike the JSON-RPC spelling of the same read. Two spellings of one operation,
  one of them off the books.
- `serve.rs:509` — the backend-leak security sweep is **silently skipped on a malformed-but-prefixed
  backend URL**, serving the vendor's real address to callers.
- `relay.rs:1221` — gRPC Trailers-Only errors are misread as unbreakable, so a permanently broken
  gRPC agent **never trips its breaker**.

Plus 11 MEDIUM including audit-chain integrity on rehydrate, two never-called retention/verification
functions permitting unbounded durable growth, a JWS multi-signature bug, and a swallowed
store-write failure.

**Three of the seven HIGH are breakers that cannot open** (`registry.rs` has no window,
`originate.rs` passes `None`, `relay.rs` misclassifies the error). A circuit breaker that cannot
trip is Law 0 applied to runtime protection rather than to a gate — and all three sit on paths that
spend money.

---

# A FAILED GENERATION IS BILLED AS A COMPLETED ONE

The codec lens closed round 3 with **15 findings, 11 HIGH**. The sharpest is money, and the proof is
the code's own enumeration.

`proto_stream.rs:117-123` defines `terminal_error` as *"the breaker-failure signal the stream-end arm
reads to distinguish a clean close from an aborted one"*, and states exactly what sets it: an
`IrStreamEvent::Error` from **an Anthropic `error` event, an OpenAI in-band `{"error":…}` frame, a
Responses `response.failed` frame, or a Bedrock in-band `*Exception` frame.**

**Four dialects are on that list. Gemini and Cohere are not** — and both have failure outcomes:
`gemini/mod.rs:1241` maps `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL` (*"model produced an unparseable tool call"*)
and Cohere maps its buffered generic `ERROR`, both to `IrStopReason::Error` — **a stop reason, not
a stream Error event.**

So the stream ends, `terminal_error` is never set, the stream-end arm reads a **clean close**, the
production billing-suppression path never fires, and **the customer is charged in full for a
generation that failed.** On two of six dialects.

## The `unwrap_or(0)` class is wider than round 2 found

Round 2 confirmed `read_count_u64(..).unwrap_or(0)` on the primary chat path. Round 3 found the same
lenient read at **two further call sites round 2 did not list**: `recover_truncated_usage` in **all
six dialects**, and `wire_shim.rs`'s `tier_usage()` cache-tier fields.

**And Bedrock's Titan-embeddings read is worse than the others.** It uses the same lenient pattern
under a comment that calls it *"BILLED COUNT"* — and there an unparseable count produces **zero
ledger rows**, not merely zero tokens. Nothing records that the work happened at all.

## Two convergent streaming defects

- **Tool-call accumulators keyed by wire index can be corrupted or silently swallow a second tool
  call** — across **Anthropic, Bedrock, Cohere and OpenAI-Chat**, by three different mechanisms
  (missing open-guards, index clamping, index reuse after close). Four dialects, one shape.
- **Cohere and OpenAI-Responses silently drop citations and annotations on the streaming path
  only** — buffered and writer both support them. **Cohere's own comments record that this exact
  bug class was already found and fixed once — on the writer side only.** The fix existed and was
  applied to one of the two paths.

## THE LENS THAT DROPPED ITS OWN FINDINGS

Two of this lens's five sub-agents exceeded scope, each re-ran a full-crate pass, and raced to write
the output file — the fourth instance of my nested-forking defect.

Its response is the standard the other lenses should be held to: it **did not trust either
sub-agent's self-verification or its file write.** It personally re-verified all 15 findings against
the pinned tree — exact `file:line`, exact quote, causal mechanism re-traced — **corrected several
line numbers**, and **dropped two candidate findings it could not verify to that standard** (a
mid-close billing claim and a moderation-flag default) rather than carry them on trust.

**Dropping a plausible finding you cannot prove is harder than reporting it, and worth more.** A
report padded with two unverified claims is not 15 findings plus 2 — it is 17 findings none of which
a reader can now trust without re-checking, which is the whole cost the audit exists to avoid.

# THE OAUTH LOGIN FLOW IS UNREACHABLE, AND THE UNBOUNDED-NUMBER SWEEP FOUND FOUR MORE

## OAuth consent is gated by a credential no browser can present

`crates/busbar-oauth2/src/routes.rs:86`, verified:

```rust
.route(id.consent_path(), RouteMethod::Get,  RouteAuth::Admin, consent_screen)
.route(id.consent_path(), RouteMethod::Post, RouteAuth::Admin, consent_submit)
```

The consent screen is **where a browser lands after the `/authorize` redirect** — it is the human
half of the flow. Both its GET and its POST are `RouteAuth::Admin`, the header-only admin
credential. The route immediately above it is `RouteAuth::None`, so this is a deliberate per-route
choice rather than a blanket default.

**A browser following a redirect cannot present an admin header.** In any deployment with a real
admin-auth chain, the OAuth login flow cannot be completed by the person it exists for.

## The unbounded-numeric sweep: one proven defect became five

I asked for every numeric field on the admin PATCH surface to be enumerated and traced, on the
strength of the single `pool_idle_timeout_secs` instance. That is what a systematic sweep is for,
and it found **four more of the same class**:

| field | where it lands | when it bites |
|---|---|---|
| `limits.upstream_request_timeout_secs` | a panicking `Instant + Duration` on the **LLM streaming hot path** (`send.rs:67`) | live |
| `health.default_probe_timeout_secs` | `busbar-llm/src/engine/health.rs:385` | **delayed fuse** — crashes the next restart's freshly-spawned prober, not immediately |
| `advanced.usage_flush_interval_ms` | `busbar-kernel/src/governance/mod.rs:1230` — unvalidated **and falsely classified as live** | **delayed fuse** — widens the governance-spend durability window after a later restart |
| `export/webhook.rs:178` | fourth instance, config-file-reachable rather than PATCH-reachable | boot |

Two of those are **delayed fuses**, which is the more dangerous shape: the operator sets a value,
the API reports success, nothing happens, and the failure arrives at the next restart — by which
time the change and the crash are separated by hours or days and by whoever restarted the process.

**The remedy is one rule, not five fixes:** every numeric on the admin surface declares a bound, and
a value that reaches an arithmetic operation, an allocation or a sleep without one is a gate failure.
That belongs in Phase 1 beside the "a comment asserting a measurement must name its command" rule.

## Two more, and the third sighting of a shipped duplicate

- **`busbar-core-admin/src/verbs.rs:303`** — a complete, shipped-but-dead **duplicate key-mint/rotate
  implementation with its own idempotency cache**. Unreachable today; a latent double-mint the
  moment anything wires a second transport to it. **Third instance of two-implementations-both-shipping**
  (after the two breakers and the two voice planes) — and this one mints credentials.
- **`busbar-core-admin/src/rate.rs:72`** — the admin rate-limit class table never gained the two
  1.6.0 named-map sections (`tools`, `agents`), so full-App-rebuild mutations on those routes get
  **6× the intended rate budget.** A 1.6.0 surface added without its 1.6.0 limit.

---

## AN ORCHESTRATION DEFECT OF MINE, NOW AT THREE INSTANCES

Three lenses have now reported that sub-agents they dispatched **ignored an explicit instruction not
to write files and wrote directly to the shared findings file** — in two cases racing, in one case
the second silently overwriting the first.

All three caught it. The resource lens re-verified twelve quotes and found a directory nothing had
read. The admin lens verified every claim itself and **corrected two factually wrong failure
scenarios** — the health-probe and usage-flush findings had been framed as immediate crashes when
the real mechanism is restart-gated, which is exactly the kind of error that makes a report sound
more alarming than the truth.

**The defect is mine, not theirs:** I told lenses to write one file and left nested forking
available, so a fork inherits "write your findings to X" and races its siblings. The fix for the
next round is structural — **one writer per file, and forks return text to their parent** — and it
goes in the round-4 brief.

The saving grace is that every lens that hit this caught it and said so unprompted. A round where
the sub-agents misbehave and the lens reports it is worth more than a round where they behave
silently, because it tells me which reports were re-verified and which were merely received.
