## OWNER PRIORITY — THE CRATE FOLD IS THE MILESTONE

Owner, 2026-09-23: *"my number one desire is crate folding to 33ish — that will be the day i feel
we have something solid in 1.6.0 vs the 40-50-60 we have now."*

Item 81 was filed in Wave 6 under "dead weight". That was wrong as a statement of priority, and it
is corrected here: **the fold is the milestone this release is measured by, and it gets its own
track rather than a line.** 49 crates today, 33 locked by #20/#39.

**The one caveat, stated plainly because it decides the order.** A fold perturbs the crate
dependency graph, and the instruments that would notice a bad fold are keyed on crate NAMES:
`kind-isolation` carries 200 `[[cell]]` and 191 `[[dep]]` rows naming crates, `package-selectors`
resolves `-p` names across CI, and several `qa/*.toml` ratchets name crates directly. Folding while
those instruments are blind means you cannot tell a good fold from one that quietly moved a byte
the oracle covers or a function the money path depends on.

**So the fold splits by risk, not by wave:**
- **CHEAP folds — start immediately, in parallel with Wave 0.** Crates outside the shipped binary's
  dependency graph, whose code the oracle cannot see and the money path does not reach. Measured:
  only 8 crates sit outside the binary, so this is the smaller half.
- **DANGEROUS folds — need the instruments first.** Any fold that merges a SHIPPING crate, moves
  code under oracle byte-identity, or touches `busbar-kernel-ledger` / `busbar-kernel-budget` /
  `crates/api`. At least 8 folds are in this class by arithmetic alone.

The fold plan — all 49 crates with verdicts, the topological order, per-fold write sets and proof
commands, risk ranking, and the parallel batches — is being computed. Until it lands, item 81
stands as the placeholder and this section is its priority statement.

## OWNER RULING 12 — EACH PLUGIN IS ONE CRATE, AND ONLY ONE. SETTLED.

Owner, 2026-09-23: *"each plugin is 1 and only 1 crate another hard rule."*

**Consequences, both directions — this rule folds AND protects:**

It REQUIRES four folds (a plugin split across crates must become one):
`busbar-llm` + `busbar-plane-llm` + `busbar-llm-codec` → one llm plane (32,834 LOC) ·
`busbar-mcp` + `busbar-plane-mcp` → one (14,258) · `busbar-a2a` + `busbar-plane-a2a` → one
(13,825) · `busbar-voice` + `busbar-voice-codec` + `busbar-plane-streaming` → one (9,976).
`busbar-plane-decision` is already compliant. **−6 crates.**

It FORBIDS the fold I had proposed: merging the six reference plugins into one
`busbar-plugin-examples`. Each is a plugin, so each is a crate. With ruling 11's seven transports,
**17 crates are now permanently protected.**

**THE ROSTER IS 37, NOT 33**, and the difference is these two rulings. 49 − 6 (plane folds) − 1
(breaker+egress → one `route` unit) − 2 (plugin infra) − 2 (timing, connsec) − 1 (delete `api`) = 37.
Going lower means deleting the reference plugins outright, which ~~removes the working proof of C2 —
one per kind —~~ is what OWNER 2026-10-03 (no test plugins) orders: the working proof of C2 is each real plugin's own CI against the published suite, not an in-busbar reference plugin; the claim was already refuted on three kinds.

## OWNER RULING 13 — NO CURRENCY SYMBOL. IT IS WRONG. FIX THE PAST.

Owner, 2026-09-23: *"no currency symbol its just wrong. figure out how to fix tha past."*

`pub currency: String` (`busbar-kernel-audit/src/record.rs:137`) is field 29 of 44 inside a SIGNED
published digest, under `DIGEST_RECIPE = "busbar.audit.digest.v1"` (`recipe.rs:37`), with a
committed golden and an ed25519 signature.

### THE MIGRATION — and why it is cheaper than this plan assumed

**Do NOT re-sign history.** A chain that can be re-signed at will is not a tamper record; it is a
document with a signature on it. The past is not broken — it is recorded under an older recipe, and
that is a different thing.

The code names its own remedy (`published_recipe_tests.rs:261`): *"Either restore the published
order or publish a NEW recipe version beside it."* So:

1. **v1 records stay valid under v1, unchanged and un-re-signed.**
2. **Cut recipe v2 without the field.** New records only.
3. **Tag each record with the recipe that produced it.** This is the piece that does not exist
   today — `record.rs` carries no recipe version — and it is what lets one verifier read both.
4. **Publish the v2 document beside v1's**, as v1 was published.
5. **Wire verification ONCE, recipe-aware.**

**Step 5 is why the cost estimate in this plan was wrong.** Item 34 priced this as "a v1 verifier
kept alive forever", which assumes a deployed verifier population to support. There isn't one: I
checked, and **nothing outside `busbar-kernel-audit` verifies the audit chain.** `verify_chain` at
`record.rs:529` and `legacy/chain.rs:302` are internal calls within the defining crate.

So we are not migrating a live verifier — **we are writing the first one, and it can be born
knowing two recipes.** Doing that is strictly cheaper than wiring a v1-only verifier now and
migrating it afterwards. **Item 34 is re-priced from "a recipe migration" to "tag the record, cut
v2, and wire the verifier that was owed anyway."**

### A FIFTH DUPLICATE, found on the way

`crates/busbar-kernel/src/calllog.rs:10` says it out loud: *"`verify_chain` — a second copy of what
other planes' provenance chains had and a **third** of what…"*. Confirmed: **three separate
`verify_chain` implementations** — `busbar-a2a/src/taskstore.rs:228` (live, called at `:601`,
`:947`), `busbar-kernel/src/calllog.rs` (live, called at `:451`), and `busbar-kernel-audit`'s own.
Three chain verifiers, three spellings of "was this record altered", **no instrument comparing
them.** Fifth instance of two-implementations-both-shipping, and the first at three.

## OWNER RULING 11 — EACH TRANSPORT IS A PLUGIN. SEVEN, NOT ONE. SETTLED.

Owner, 2026-09-23, verbatim: *"each transport is a plugin i set that rule and not to be discussed
again so we have 7 not 1 transports."*

**The seven transport crates are OFF the fold table permanently.** `http`, `ws`, `tls`, `grpc`,
`sse`, `stdio`, `tcp` — 5,534 lines across seven crates — each is a plugin instance of the
`transport` kind, exactly as the five planes are instances of the `plane` kind. Any future proposal
to merge them is refused by this ruling without argument. Do not re-open it; do not "measure whether
it would help".

### WHAT THE RULING COSTS — because the code does not have a transport kind

The ruling makes explicit something that was previously only a gap. If each transport is a plugin,
then each must satisfy C2 — compiled in OR dropped in, over the same contract. **Today not one of
them can be, because the plugin machinery has SIX kinds and `transport` is not among them.**
Verified, all three layers:

| layer | what it holds |
|---|---|
| `busbar-plugin/src/cold/mod.rs:90-109` | the kind constants: `STORE`, `SECRET`, `AUTH`, `HOOK`, `EXPORT`, `PLANE` — **six** |
| `plugin-loader/src/sign.rs:70` | `KNOWN_KINDS = ["store","auth","hook","secret","export","plane"]` — **six** |
| `plugin-loader/src/registry.rs:43` | `supported_abi` matches per kind and has **no transport arm** |

And `sign.rs:607` **hard-refuses any manifest whose `kind` is outside `KNOWN_KINDS`** — so a
`kind: transport` plugin cannot be signed, let alone loaded.

**The spec says seven kinds (Part 2 #3). The code implements six.** That is not a ratchet or a
count; it is a kind that exists in the architecture and not in the binary.

### This explains three findings that looked unrelated

- **`construction/rules2.rs:314`** lists six kinds and omits `transport`, so `manifest-allowlist`
  emits 14 rows and **no transport row at all** — green while thirteen crates breach the dep wall.
  It was not an oversight in the gate; the gate matches the code's six-kind reality.
- **`INSTANCE_VOCAB_KINDS = ["plane","transport"]`** — the C1 gate measures a transport kind the
  plugin system does not have. One of its two axes is aimed at something unimplemented.
- **`plugins.yaml` expresses 4 of 7 kinds.** The registry cannot express `transport` because nothing
  downstream could load one.

### The ordering this forces

Widening `plugins.yaml` or the kind vocabulary to accept `transport` **before** the loader, signer
and ABI can carry one would create a registry naming a kind no binary can load — a new false-green
surface of exactly the class those registries exist to close. **The kind must land in
`cold/mod.rs`, `KNOWN_KINDS` and `supported_abi` first**, then the registry, then the seven crates'
manifests. That sequence is now a Phase 4 item with a stated order, not a loose set of gaps.

## OWNER RULINGS OWED

~~**Item 21 — the overcharge.**~~ **STRUCK — my false conclusion.** I read `UsageLedger`'s field
doc and then indicted a `MeteringDelta` writer on a different path; the refund is live at
`ingress/mod.rs:671-676`. I had escalated this as inverting ruling 8. It does not. Corrected in
`1a642eb1d`; the entry is struck here because the plan contradicted itself, saying "NO OWNER RULING
IS OWED" in one place and listing it here in another.

**Each of the following needs a decision, not a measurement. I have changed nothing on any of them.**

1. **Item 15 — `main`'s 7 divergent commits.** Only an owner protection change resolves it; no code
   fix exists. `enforce_admins: true`, `allow_force_pushes: false`, and `qa` is not a descendant.
2. **Item 34 — the audit-chain `currency` field** is field 29 of 44 inside a SIGNED published
   digest with a committed golden and an ed25519 signature. Removing it is recipe v2 + per-record
   tagging + a re-derived golden + a v1 verifier kept alive — not a field deletion. The code names
   its own remedy at `published_recipe_tests.rs:262`.
3. **THE RATE-CARD MONEY CELL — is the new figure the intended behaviour?**
   `billing|rate-card|history-mid-window` moves `spend_final` 750,090,000 → 277,530,000 µ, caused
   by `root-llm` alone (2×2 isolated, no interaction). Two things make this a decision rather than
   a defect report: the measured triple is digit-for-digit the **priced-at-charge** answer the
   cell's own `why` names, so it looks deliberate — **but that same text says ONLY the final read
   should move, and three reads moved** (`by_key`, `by_model`, `total`). If intended, D-3 needs
   `effects.script` added to its classes AND the two earlier reads explained. If not, it is a
   money defect on the revenue plane.
4. **THE LEDGER 404 → 403 — accepted or not?** `ledger|amend|adjusting-entries` and
   `|refused-unsigned` move `not_found` → `forbidden`, caused by `root-admin` alone. **The
   register's own differ already has a rule for this**: a `status` change requires a `breaking`
   entry with a changelog line. D-4 covers `body` and `headers` and not `status`, so the rule was
   never reached. Accepting it means writing that entry; refusing it means the status is wrong.
5. **THE KEY-USAGE CELL — the exception ruling 8 already implies, but nobody wrote.**
   `billing|key-usage|after-upstream-down` moves `spend 250 → 0`, and the cell's own `why` says **0
   is correct** — an upstream that served nothing owes nothing. That is ruling 8's shape exactly
   (C4 beats C3 where 1.5.5's bytes are wrong), so it needs a **signed C3 exception**, not a fix.
   The missing exception is the defect.

# THE WAVES — 394 items, 100 slots, and the partition PROVED


A **slot** is one agent's work. A **wave** is every slot in a phase; they run simultaneously.
**Phases run in order.**

## The partition, proved rather than asserted (Law 8)

**One DIRECTORY = one slot.** A directory's `mod.rs` therefore has exactly one owner, so the
slot that adds an exit test can declare its new module without racing a neighbour. A directory
larger than 14 items splits **by file**, and the split is declared in the table — the first
sub-slot owns the directory's shared module files.

**The phase is a property of the DIRECTORY, not the item.** A directory travels whole, in the
earliest phase any of its items demands. The earlier attempt assigned phase per item, which put
one directory in two phases and handed one `mod.rs` to two slots — measured, 13 files collided.

Re-measured after the fix:

| check | result |
|---|---|
| work items placed | **394 of 394** |
| duplicate item ids | **0** |
| files claimed by more than one slot | **0** |
| directories split without a declared owner | **0** |
| largest slot | **14 items** |

## Model per slot

| model | assigned when | slots |
|---|---|--:|
| **opus** | the slot touches **money** or **the oracle**, or carries any **HIGH** | 67 |
| **sonnet** | worst severity is **MEDIUM** | 19 |
| **haiku** | all **LOW / NIT** | 14 |

**Money is matched on the defect statement, not only the path.** The path tokens
(`ledger`, `cost`, `budget`, `price`, `meter`, `settle`, `hold`, `audit`) miss read-time money —
an item that changes what a customer is charged while living in a file whose name says nothing
about money. The text match adds *billed · invoice · charged · priced · rate-card · spend ·
token-count · metered · overdraft · refund · settles · unpriced*. That reclassification moved
**56 items** into the money tier, including three that the path-only rule had sitting on haiku.

## ITEM 572 — `oauth-as` 0.9.3 → 1.0.0, and it runs BEFORE the waves

**Owner-added. This is not an audit finding; it is a dependency the release must carry.**

| | |
|---|---|
| pinned today | `oauth-as = "0.9.3"` (`Cargo.toml:201`) |
| latest | **1.0.0**, published 2026-09-24 |
| versions crossed | 0.9.5 · 0.10.0 · 0.11.0 · 0.11.1 · 0.11.2 · **1.0.0** |
| `cargo update` would give | 0.9.5 — semver-compatible only, **not** what is wanted |
| features enabled | `http` · `jwt` · `consent` · `resource-metadata`, plus `test-util` in two dev-deps |
| consumers | `busbar-oauth2` · `busbar-kernel` (both normal and dev) |
| call sites | **93** non-comment `oauth_as::` references across `crates/` |

**Why it is a serialization point and not a slot.** Under 0.x, every minor is a breaking change,
so this crosses **three** breaking boundaries before the major. It edits the workspace root
`Cargo.toml` (owned by nobody) *and* the manifests and call sites of `busbar-oauth2` and
`busbar-kernel` — directories owned by W2.1–W2.3, W4.10 and W4.11. Running it as a parallel slot
would violate §4.2 outright.

So it does not run in a wave. **It runs alone, before wave 0**, and every other slot builds on
top of the result. A dependency bump that changes what compiles is not something ninety-nine
agents should discover one at a time.

**Model: `opus`.** It is not money, but `oauth_as` mints tokens, and §9.5 holds customer-visible
behaviour identical to 1.5.5. A major bump of the authorization server is exactly where an
OAuth-visible change enters without anyone deciding to make one.

**Done means:**

1. `oauth-as = "1.0.0"` in `Cargo.toml`, lockfile regenerated, `cargo check --workspace` clean.
2. **Every one of the 93 call sites re-verified** — a bump that compiles is not a bump that is
   correct. Compilation proves the shapes match, not that the semantics did not move.
3. The feature list re-derived against 1.0.0's own feature table, not carried across verbatim.
   Features are renamed and split across a major.
4. **The upstream changelog read, and every behavioural change between 0.9.3 and 1.0.0 written
   into the slot-log row** — named, not summarised. This is the step that catches a
   customer-visible change before a customer does.
5. Any behavioural change that reaches a client is **queued per §1.8**, not absorbed. It is a
   §9.5 decision and it is the owner's.
6. Exit test: the OAuth conformance leg green on the new version, plus a test that pins one
   observable the bump could plausibly have moved — proved RED against 0.9.3 per §16.

no slot and no agent should open them — doing so burns a model on a file that is going away and
manufactures a merge conflict against the fold.

They are recorded, not deleted: if the fold changes, they return to the work.

# THE LIST, CARRIED — the retired `1.6.0-THE-LIST-GENERATED.md` and `1.6.0-THE-LIST.md` §1, by phase

Carried 2026-09-27 (SPEC-ABSORB) before those two files were deleted. Every row below was re-checked
against today's tree: its symbol (or, where it named none, its file) still exists, and `where` is
the location found today, not the one the row was filed at. The id is the row's original id and is
stable; the row's full evidence and action are in git history at the file's last commit. `class/`
carries the original certainty: a **PARK** row needs an owner ruling or is a billed byte and is
never self-approved (#10/#59); **ADJUDICATE** means the finding must be re-derived before it is
worked. Rows overlap some numbered items above (a symbol can carry more than one finding); an
agent working a numbered item closes the matching row here in the same commit.

| phase | rows | table |
|---|--:|---|
| PHASE 1 — instruments | 136 | → PHASE 1 |
| PHASE 2 — money | 113 | → PHASE 2 |
| PHASE 3 — C3 (customer surface, config) | 60 | → PHASE 3 |
| PHASE 4 — C1/C2 (missing code, ABI) | 129 | → PHASE 4 |
| SECURITY (auth; with items 144-153) | 49 | → SECURITY |
